# Cycle 0045: carried defects

Part 2 of [Issue #85](https://github.com/laststance/codebook/issues/85) and of [the cycle record](0045-natural-request-and-carried-defects.md): every defect [Cycle 0044](0044-wiki-update-and-carried-defects.md#next-action) left unfixed, copied in whole. Under the [cycle rule](../../AGENTS.md#cycle-rule-every-carried-defect-is-fixed-in-the-next-cycle) every row is a task; the cycle closes only when the **Closed** column names the fix and its test, or the evidence, on every row.

The first column is the finding's label in Cycle 0044 and the second its text there (for the labels A-L, B-L, C-L, D-L, B-M, C-M and D-M: the text of the local review of 2026-10-04; for DOC-1 to DOC-6: what the delivery's documentation audit reported). State, evidence and "Closes with" come from the intake of 2026-10-04, a read-only pass against `main` `6a7a0b8` in five groups; nothing was run, so a test described as red today is a reading of the source until its first run. "Closes with" is the row's acceptance criterion in Issue #85. Where a plan review of 2026-10-04 changed what closes a row, the cell ends with **Amended by:** and the amendments (DX the devex, DR the design, ER the engineering review; "ER 1.11" is that review's list of rows whose named test cannot be red on today's code; PC the Codex challenge of the merged plan; X7, CR and "RF-1 b" the outside read of the 0.2.28.0 range); their text is in [the plan review amendments](0045-plan-review-amendments.md), and the amended wording is the criterion.

Totals at intake: 222 rows; OPEN 215, UNKNOWN 6 (W-6, NF-8, NF-11, NF-15, NF-27, T-3), NOT-A-DEFECT 1 (AD-2), CLOSED 0.

Labels of Cycle 0044 without a row of their own, because each is another row: SM-11 is SEC-3, SM-23 is SEC-5, CX-D2 is SD-4, CX-A2 is NF-29, CX-S2 is RT-4. Pairs kept as two rows that one fix closes: C-L3 with B-M3, A-L1 with AD-13, A-L5 with AD-14, C-L6 with CX-A3, C-L2 with AD-12, B-L7 with the second half of AD-15.

### Notes of the intake, group 1

Counts: 39 OPEN, 5 UNKNOWN (W-6, NF-8, NF-11, NF-15, NF-27), no CLOSED, no NOT-A-DEFECT.

Rows that are the same defect or share one cause

- NF-11 and NF-27: the same code path and the same deciding observation (one in each host). Not shown to be one defect.
- The wait at `studyWorkspace.ts:2001` ("reading context before capture") is NF-19's defect. It is also what the "later restore" of NF-21 and the Arrange command of NF-10 ran into; those two rows keep their own cause (the sticky `liveComposition` flag, the grid that is not applied again).
- NF-26's fourth focus state (reader closed after the run) is NF-21.
- NF-5 (at normal panel height), NF-6 and NF-7 may share one cause, the identity of an editor group across Cursor's wrapper rebuilds. This is a hypothesis; one grid and event dump in Cursor decides all three.
- NF-15's Cursor half and NF-8 may both be NF-16's paint loss on the WSLg desktop. The two commits of NF-16 would not fix NF-8.
- NF-1 and the first reveal of NF-4 concern the same learner tab in the Application group and need one owner decision; the code paths differ. NF-17's second observation is NF-4 if it shows `BODY`.
- NF-22 and NF-26 both concern a kept non-text tab; the fixes use the same naming utility (`foreignPreviewObstacle`, `editorTabName`).

Rows whose described cause the source does not support

- W-8: the candidate does not refuse the next start "until it is deleted by hand". `selectActiveWikiCandidate.ts:34` stops counting a candidate 15 minutes after its last write. The defect is the 15 minutes without a way to say that the chat was stopped.
- W-4: nothing in Codebook causes the seven approvals. They are the chat's own for files outside the workspace, and the output was outside only because of the misread sentence that `242aa6d` corrected.
- NF-12: the N-11 fix added the text and the 10 s bound, but the text still reaches only the playback state; the status bar command shows nothing.
- NF-13 and NF-23 are recorded as Cursor findings; both causes are in Codebook's own code and apply to both hosts.
- NF-15: both halves were seen on candidate 6, before the delivered fix; they have not been seen on 0.2.28.
- NF-28: the blank-line limit is not a separate rule. It follows from the bound "column at most line length + 1", and the refusal text does not name the column.
- NF-30: not introduced by `5180294`; the close rule never covered a capture that was not the immediately preceding source.
- COV-1: for a Wiki published before file hashes were recorded (no `ownedFiles`) the start check names no page, so after the fix such an update starts instead of being refused.

Dependencies

- W-2, W-4 and W-7 are one native run in VS Code (a generation, then one update with video). W-9 can use the candidate folder of W-8's reproduction. W-1 needs the Cursor Agent CLI.
- NF-9 depends on NF-25 (the same offer). NF-10's native check needs NF-5's collapsed state. NF-29 (d) and the 0041-N10-1b part of NF-18 are one chat run, which the cycle's planned natural request can supply only if its declaration names an unmapped line.
- The fix of NF-21 changes which branch a select takes after a close; NF-19's test must be written with it in place.

Limits of this intake

- Nothing was built or run. Causes marked "read from the source" or "hypothesis" are not confirmed by a run.
- Statements about the hosts (how a status entry's command is converted in NF-23, how long a toast stays in W-6 and NF-25, the 500 ms cancellation in NF-3) come from the editor's source or bundle and from the QA logs under `/home/murakami/codebook-qa`, not from a new observation.
- The NF rows other than NF-12, NF-17 to NF-19, NF-22 and NF-23 were read by three delegated read-only readers; the lines cited from their reports were checked again against the tree.
- Test titles are proposals. `configuredPreparation.test.ts` as the place of NF-28's second test was not checked against that file's content.

### Notes of the intake, group 2

Rows that are the same defect or the same code:

- T-2 and T-7 share one item: the local CodeRabbit CLI was not run (owner sign-in). Their other halves differ (plan reviews without an outside voice; no outside model read the whole delivery).
- SM-16 and SM-17 are one sentence kept in two files (`src/resources.ts:148`, `extension/README.md:242`); one rewording, two tests.
- ST-15 and SM-18 are about the same function (`refuseSlidePastDeclaredLine`): ST-15 is the missing test, SM-18 the wrong names and advice. One test serves both, with SM-18's text as the expected value.
- ST-21 and SM-19 meet in one string (`ownedRepositoryBreakpoint.ts:304`): the naming SM-19 describes is what makes ST-21's text wrong.
- ST-13 is the test-side symptom of D-M1 (the fake clock adds real time), which is not in this group; one fix in `src/tests/utils/fakeClock.ts` closes both.
- RF-1's leftover directory is the second clause of AD-6 (not in this group).
- ST-14, ST-15, ST-16, ST-19, ST-21 and SM-16 to SM-22 all come from the fix `bb9e055` of row 0041-N10-1b and touch the same few places (`armStop`, `refuseSlidePastDeclaredLine`, `createBindingLineRule`, `isSavedBindingReceiptAtStop`); doing them together avoids editing the same expected texts twice.

Rows whose described cause differs from the source:

- RF-1 says "an empty directory". The leftover is the prepared directory `writer.lock.takeover-<uuid>`; it is empty only when the window dies between the `mkdir` (:49) and the `writeFile` (:51), otherwise it holds the owner file. So a sweep can judge most leftovers with `isStaleStorageLock` on the owner file and needs an age rule only for an empty one (a live window is in that state for an instant).
- T-1 says 519 on `main`. The baseline committed at `c549da6` sums to 524 `crap_*` findings; 597 on today's tree is confirmed. Files with new or more findings: 34; the row names eight functions only, so the fix has to take the list from the baseline diff (`git diff c549da6 6a7a0b8 -- extension/fallow-health-baseline.json`).
- T-4's 3 s is `CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/runtimeSupport/constants.ts:3`, not `REPOSITORY_ADAPTER_PAUSE_LIMIT_MS` (also 3 s).
- ST-8 names the deferred guard. The same message is thrown by a second guard (`configuredBrowserRun.ts:1697`, the ordinary refresh); tests reach that one (`nativeParticipants.test.ts:423` asserts `status === 'failed'`) but none asserts the message of either.
- SM-9 says three sets. Two sets classify error kinds (`UNRETRYABLE_READER_ERROR_KINDS`, `CHOICE_RETIRED_ERROR_KINDS`); the third nearby set, `CONTROL_RETIRING_MESSAGE_TYPES`, holds pane message types, not error kinds.
- ST-19 says the browser harness cannot express a later stop on an unmapped line. Its `verifyBreakpointsOnlyOnLines` already applies to every owned breakpoint (`configuredRuntimeHarness.ts:264-268`); what it lacks is a pause of a later stop at the requested place (:432 serves the first stop's input pause only).
- SM-2: no behavior differs today. `renewPauseHold` also requires a `paused-` status, and no other deadline lasts 300 s, so the row is a latent coupling, and its test needs the two constants made equal.

Dependencies:

- ST-20 depends on the product fix of NF-29 (first clause) and CX-A2; without that fix the corrected test is red and shows the native loss.
- ST-15's expected text depends on whether SM-18's advice is changed in the same commit.
- SM-17's test depends on SM-16's wording.
- T-2, T-7: the owner's `coderabbit auth login`. RF-1 (Windows part): a Windows host with Node. T-3: the Mac-private review log of PR #54 or the owner's word.
- T-5 needs the two signed-in QA editors (present on this host per the Cycle 0044 record) and Copilot credits; on exhaustion the standing rule names the Codex Extension as the fallback, recorded as such.
- Rows closed by comments or docstrings alone (SM-13, SM-15, SM-20, parts of SM-19 and SM-22) have no test that can fail on today's source. Each cell names the evidence to record instead (a grep, the diff, `tsc`). The cycle rule closes a row by "a fix with a test that fails without it" or by evidence that it is not a defect; a corrected comment fits neither exactly, so the Issue should state that recorded evidence closes these rows.

### Notes of the intake, group 3

Method. Read-only: source, tests and the Cycle 0044 record were read at `6a7a0b8`; nothing was built or run. Every line number above was checked against the current tree. All 43 rows are OPEN; none was found fixed.

Where the tests live. The Study pane's script has no unit seam: `extension/src/tests/studyHtml.test.ts` only checks strings of the HTML. Pane behaviour (focus, layout, ARIA) is checked by the named cases of `extension/scripts/study-bootstrap-qa.mjs`, which `reader-dom.yml` runs in real Chrome; "DOM case" above means a new case there. The Wiki page script is tested on a fake page in `extension/src/tests/wikiUpdatePage.test.ts`, which records the reader's requests.

Same defect.

- SD-4 and CX-D2 (CX-D2 is in another group) are one defect.
- SP-7, SS-12 and SS-13 are one fix (one scan per stop, handed to every reader); each row keeps its own criterion.
- SS-9 and ST-9 (other group) are one change: SS-9 is the wrapper, ST-9 the behaviour tests of the call sites.

Causes found different from the row.

- SP-1: when `visibilitychange` and `focus` arrive together, the first look is abandoned at `installWikiUpdateStatus.ts:412` before its freshness read, so that pair normally costs two status reads and one inspection, not two. The defect that remains is one full inspection per return with no interval, and two when the second return arrives during the first one's freshness read.
- SD-7: `#study-unopenable` is not a model to copy; it has the same gap (no role, not referenced).
- SS-8: there are five call sites, all with one argument; the tests reach the macOS branch by mocking `process.platform`, so the parameter is not a test seam either.
- SS-9: beside the ten wrapped calls there are direct non-modal calls at 316, 490, 645 to 648 and 652 (static text, or text `wikiUpdateNotice` already cleaned).
- CX-D1: the note names a button that `studyHtml.ts:1431` hides unless a retryable error is shown. Not checked: whether a repairable entry always arrives with such an error.
- SP-5: the first `readMainEditorGrid` also hands off the source focus, so the reorder must show that hand-off still happens once (the record's confidence was 4).

Behaviour-preserving simplifications. For SS-1, SS-3, SS-5, SS-10, SS-11, SS-13, SS-14 and SS-8 no behaviour test can be red on the unfixed code. Each is closed by the fix plus recorded evidence (the duplicate is gone, the named existing tests are green), which the cycle rule allows as "no longer applies". SS-2, SS-7 and SS-15 have a test that is red or does not compile today. SS-4 and SS-6 get a test for a path no test executes today; it is green before and after the fix and red under the named mutation.

Dependencies.

- SP-3 needs a harness that answers `Debugger.getPossibleBreakpoints` (ST-1, ST-15).
- SS-6 touches the rule ST-12 tests; SS-8 touches the docstring of SM-13.
- SD-3, SD-6, SD-7 and CX-D1 all change the Saved studies section (`studyHtml.ts:159`, `constants.ts:139`); SD-8, SD-15 and CX-D5 all change Show Variables; SD-11, SD-13 and CX-D4 all change the reload button. Each group is best done as one change.
- Existing tests with exact strings that these fixes change: `studyHtml.test.ts` (hint, CX-D5), `wikiUpdatePage.test.ts` lines 665 and 963 (SD-11, CX-D3), `wikiReaderShell.test.ts` (SD-13).
- Rows read from code only (SD-6, SD-15, SD-17, SP-6): the code path is present; the first run of the new DOM case or test is also the first observation of the defect.

### Notes of the intake, group 4

Rows that are the same defect

- B-M3 is C-L3 (another group): one fix, the two tests named in B-M3.
- RT-4 is CX-S2, RT-12 is NF-28, AD-12 is C-L2, the second half of AD-15 is B-L7 (all in other groups). SEC-3 is SM-11 and SEC-5 is SM-23 by their own labels.
- AD-1 generalises C-M2: C-M2's backup folder is one source of "added" files; each keeps its own test.
- CX-A3 generalises C-L6. D-M1 is the mechanism of ST-13.
- Inside this group, shared code: RT-11 and AD-6 (the lock file and its owner; RT-11's complete lock write is what makes AD-6's "release only your own lock" safe, so RT-11 goes first). AD-4 and CX-A3 (one `lstat` walk of the output folder in the start check). AD-7 and C-M2 (the publisher's `.previous-` backup name, one constant). RT-3, AD-3, C-M1 and AD-1 all change `classifyWikiUpdateEnd.ts`. AD-9 and CX-A4 both change the watch (`waitForWikiPublish.ts`, `readWikiWatchStamp.ts`). RT-10 and AD-10 both need a run identity.

Rows whose described cause differs from the source

- AD-7: the receipts of the backups are not parsed every 2 s. `readWikiWatchStamp` gates each look, so the parse runs only on a look that follows a write; what runs every 2 s is one `stat` per sibling folder's receipt and manifest. Both grow with each update.
- AD-5: the abort is rechecked, but only after `spawn` (`runCursorWikiUpdate.ts:104`). So the agent process does start, is signalled at once, and still receives the prompt on stdin (:152). The timer tie is as recorded.
- AD-2: established by reading, not left open: the same column bound has run on every committed stop since v0.2.0.0, so no saved study can violate the new rule.
- SEC-5: the two rules also disagree for a binding on the declared line itself when the breakpoint was requested later (bound D, requested R), not only for lines strictly between.
- D-M1: the replay test's line numbers drifted (now 2039-2064: 3,000 + 1,750 fake ms against a 5,000 ms bound leave the 250 ms). D-M4: all three copies were read; two flatten the layout, one keeps it.
- AD-12: the swallowed release is confirmed. The "returns as a success" path was not re-derived by reading (a stderr held open also holds the child's `close`), so the new test has to reproduce that case first.
- RT-3: two existing tests assert today's answer (`classifyWikiUpdateEnd.test.ts:175`, `wikiUpdateFlow.test.ts:438`), so the fix changes pinned behavior.

Dependencies

- Needs a host this machine cannot produce: RT-6 and RT-7 (an editor for Windows in a WSL remote window, NF-18's N-1), AD-6 (macOS, for the host name part only).
- Needs an owner choice: C-M1 (may a folder without Git end as "Updated"), RT-1 (must 0.2.28 studies open in 0.2.27), RT-7 (the way out where there is no Trash), AD-3 (may Update run the publisher's recovery in Cursor).
- D-M2 needs its native observation before the harness changes; D-M4's target layout follows that observation if VS Code flattens.
- Tests that are rewritten, not only added: RT-3 (two, above), AD-11 (`findWikiUpdateStartBlock.test.ts:40`). SEC-3 keeps the rejection that `extension.test.ts:1070` asserts; only its message changes.
- Nothing was run: every state is from reading the tree at 6a7a0b8.

### Notes of the intake, group 5

Counts: 43 rows, all OPEN. No row is CLOSED, NOT-A-DEFECT or UNKNOWN: every described state is in the tree at `6a7a0b8`. Three rows are marked inferred (C-L1, C-L6, C-L8): the code path is as described, but the condition that makes it bite (storage shared by two machines, a link under the Wiki folder, a remote host in another time zone) was read, not seen.

Rows that are the same defect as another label of the Cycle 0044 record:

- A-L1 = AD-13. AD-13 names only the nine-session half; the rejected `close()` that answers the new client with an error is in A-L1 alone.
- A-L5 = AD-14.
- B-L7 = the second half of AD-15 (its first half, the unpinned integrity of `@playwright/cli`, is not in this group).
- C-L2 = AD-12.

- B-L1: COV-1 (W-10, the deleted `index.html`) is one instance of this row; the same fix closes it if the catch covers `currentDeepWiki()`.

- D-L2 belongs to the family of D-M1 and ST-13 (the fake clock); D-L1 and D-L3 are separate helpers.
- DOC-1 and DOC-4 both rewrite the lock passage and the "Recorded walkthroughs" passage of `extension/README.md`; write them together.

Rows whose description needed a correction against the source:

- A-L2: the dead argument is real, and no behaviour depends on it. What is missing is a test of the reachable half of that branch (the refusal "A breakpoint still targets the selected position; owned removal cannot retire learner breakpoints." is asserted by no test).

- C-L8: besides the notification, a host-formatted time is inside a refusal reason that the page itself shows (the candidate's time at `extension.ts:856`).
- D-L3: more exactly, a timer set by a callback is counted from the end of the advance, not from the time its callback was due; `now()` inside callbacks has the same error.
- DOC-5: the count of straight-apostrophe titles is 13 (found by comparing all 2,422 test and DOM-case titles with the file). DOC-6: checked and wrong, as described in the row.

## Rows opened again after the second native round, 2026-10-04

A Closed cell that a native observation contradicts does not count. These two rows are open again; each cell is written anew when the fix named here is merged. The first closure is kept below as it was written.

- **NF-6** (open again). Check 2 of the checks owed on candidate 14 (Cursor 3.23.12, `owed14/results.json`): a live entry from the select's three-pane layout still ends with `layoutConfirmed` false and no receipt (the context pane in column 3 and its group are closed at the entry, and five events replace every group object); from the Recording's two-pane layout it ends true. The row's closing condition, `layoutConfirmed` true at ready on a live entry from the Study layout, does not hold. Closes with: the second part of package S1, a test of the entry from the three-pane layout that is red on `e124f43`, and the same native check on the final candidate. First closure: FIXED, package S1, commit `80759dd,` bb01f43, merged in `6e8044e`: Observation (ER-30, obs13 item 8, Cursor 3.23.12): at ready no layout receipt exists; five `onDidChangeTabGroups` events named every group and each replaced every `TabGroup` object; the tab closes were applied to the existing objects first (`live3.events.txt` +448 ms and +510 ms: the same object numbers before and after the close), and the emptied Study group was removed 19 ms after its tab closed (+529 ms). Two causes on the source: (1) `arrange` knew the retired Study group only by its object (`grid.groups.at(-1) === retiredContextGroup`), which is another object after any such event, so the arrangement that measured the still present, empty group answered "Existing editor groups were preserved. Use the focus commands or arrange the Codebook panes manually." and took no receipt; (2) a group whose tabs have no known input (the chat group) and a group without tabs got a new identity with every such event, which fails `sameEditorGroups` for every receipt and guard that spans one. (The package's closure notes hold the rest.)
- **D-M2** (open again). ## Rows that wait for a native observation

- **NF-17** (open). Package S2 changed no code for it: the fix DR-20 words needs to know that the Learning summary's document held the keyboard focus when its view became hidden, and the editor gives a `WebviewView` only its visibility. Two observations decide the row and are made natively by the driving agent before the final candidate: (1) with the keyboard focus in the Learning summary, hide the summary's view and read `document.activeElement` of the workbench and where the next key goes; (2) the same with the focus in a chat or a terminal. When (1) shows that the editor already moves the focus to a usable place, the row closes by that evidence with a pin; when the focus is left on the page body, the summary's document reports its focus to the host (package PN) and the host moves the focus to the active editor group only then. Package S2's note: NATIVE-PENDING, package S2, commit `none`, merged in `fd95df6`: The row closes by a native observation that has not been made (it is not in the cycle record's table of candidate 13 nor in the second round), and DR-20's fix is conditional on its result. Observation needed, both hosts, `document.activeElement` logged every 40 ms: (1) "Codebook: Show Learning Summary", then close its side bar: within 1 s the focus is in an editor or a Codebook pane, not `BODY`. (2) Restart the extension host with the reader open, click the dead Application tab, start a run without a presentation call: the log from the start to the first capture (a `BODY` there is NF-4, package S1). Without `BODY` in (1) the row closes by the log (DR-20). With `BODY`, DR-20's fix cannot be built in the host alone; see "Conflicts with the plan". Nothing of the row is independent of the observation, so no code was changed.
